# Bhagavad-gita commentary by Bhaktivinoda Thakura

## Chapter One - Viṣāda-yoga

May Kṛṣṇa Caitanya candra remain ever excellent!

By the power of Pañca-tattva’s mercy, the commentator Vidyābhūṣaṇa revealed the sweet moon of *Gītā’s* truth that was covered by the cloud of Māyāvāda and spread its full bliss on earth.

Following his commentary, lowly Bhaktivinoda has written *Vidvad rañjana* to explain his commentary on the sweetness of *Gītā*, following his commentary, and offers respects to the pure devotee.

Advaita prabhu was a great devotee of the jewel of the *Gītā*. I offer respects on the ground to him. Giving mercy to this servant of the Lord, holding his feet on my head, giving power to me, fulfil my desire.

Merciful to the *jīvas* of this world, he brought Gaura-hari and taught the essence of the *tattva* of *Gītā*. If I attain his mercy, I can cross the ocean of *tattva*. What doubt is there about this?

O Gaura! O Nityānanda! O Advaita, full of *prema!* O Lakṣmī-priyā and Viṣṇu-priyā! O Gadādhara! O Jāhnavā! O Vaṁśivadanānanda! O Rūpa! O Sanātana! O Svarupa Dāmodara! O Rāmānanda! O Śrīvāsa! O Śrīdhara!

I am most fallen and wretched! Your mercy is complete. You can bestow perfection to the fool. Mercifully destroy all obstacles, reveal all the *tattvas* and give me the power to write this commentary.

With the intention of delivering the faithful *jīvas* from the mouth of the tiger of ignorance, on the pretext of removing Arjuna’s illusion, the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, taught the *Gītā*, which defines the nature of *ātmā*. The five topics of *īśvara* (the Lord), *jīva, prakṛti, kāla* (time) and karma are clearly deliberated on in the *Gītopaniṣad*. Among them, the *īśvara* is the great conscious entity, the *jīva* is the minute conscious entity, matter which shelters *sattva, rajas* and *tamas* is *prakṛti,* special material substance devoid of the three *guṇas* is time and the fate produced by the *jīva’s* actions is called karma. In this way the characteristics of these five are described. *Īśvara*, *jīva, prakṛti* and time are eternal. *Jīva, prakṛti* and time are dependent on *īśvara*. Karma is without beginning but may have an end. *Īśvara* and *jīva*, both having consciousness (*saṁvit*) as their nature, are knowers and have a sense of ‘I’. The sense of ‘I’ in *īśvara* and *jīva* is spiritual *ahaṅkāra*. It is not the *ahaṅkāra* generated from *mahat-tattva*. When the *jīva* is within *prakṛti,* the *ahaṅkāra* born of *mahat-tattva,* arising from *prakṛti* takes shelter of the *jīva*. When the *jīva* becomes free of *prakṛti,* that *ahaṅkāra* merges in *prakṛti* and does not associate with the liberated *jīva*. *Īśvara* and *jīva* are both doers (*kartā*) and experiencers (*bhoktā*). *Bhoktā* means an experiencer (rather than an enjoyer). Even though being a knower is accomplished from *saṁvit*, like illumination comes from the sun, the illuminator, still *saṁvit* and the knower manifest as different entities by distinction of *saṁvit* and the person employing *saṁvit*. There is no difference between them in actuality, but eternal distinguishable qualities become particular *tattvas* with different *svarūpas*.

Thus, the *Gītā* teaches the highest *tattva* in the form of eternal *acintya-bhedābheda*. Even in the absence of difference, there is perception of difference. This is an eternal principle. Internal (*svagata*) difference, arising in the state of quality and possessor of the quality is eternally unavoidable. The finer deliberation on all these subjects may be seen in appropriate places in the *Gītā*.

In this scripture the *jīva,* Paramātmā, his abode, and all the means of attaining him are described suitably. The *jīva* in his true state and suitability of Paramātmā, the suitability of worshipping Paramātmā, and *prakṛti,* time and karma as the instruments of Paramātmā, the creator, are described.

There are three methods of attaining the truth: *karma, jñāna* and *bhakti*. When the heart is purified by performance of *sva-dharma* while giving up desire for results and the sense of being the doer, it assists the practice of *jñāna* and *bhakti*. Thus, *karma* is accepted as a *sādhana,* when related in this sequence.

*Karma* is of two types: primary and secondary. Primary *karma* avoids violence as prescribed in scriptures. Secondary *karma* performs violence as prescribed in scripture. With purification of the heart by *karma, jñāna* arises. When one is endowed with *jñāna,* it matures into *bhakti.*

When one searches out the one conscious entity by a slight glance, it is called *jñāna*. By that one attains *sālokya, sārūpya, sāmīpya, sārṣṭi* and *sāyujya*. When this *jñāna* matures, and one begins to search with unblinking glance, the highest *puruṣārtha* in the form of service, etc., to the Lord appears through pure *bhakti* which includes *sālokya*, etc., taking shelter of various spiritual pastimes within the single conscious *tattva*. This is the only difference between *bhakti* and *jñāna*.

In the first six chapters of *Gītā*, knowledge of the *jīva* as an *aṁśa* of the *īśvara*, and the *svarūpa* of the *īśvara*, the *aṁśī*, suitable for worship, achieved through *niṣkāma-karma,* is shown. In the middle six chapters, there are seen teachings of *bhakti* which gives attainment of the highest goal along with *bhakti’s* glorification. In the last six chapters surrender in pure *bhakti* is shown as the conclusion while describing with clarity the conclusion concerning *īśvara* and other topics which were previously discussed.

The person who has controlled his senses and is fixed in the proper dharma with faith is qualified for this scripture. There are three types of qualified persons: *saniṣṭha, pariniṣṭhita* and *nirapekṣa*. The person who faithfully performs his *sva-dharma* in the form of worshipping the Lord while having desire to see Svarga-loka is the *saniṣṭha*. The person who is absorbed in serving *bhakti* while performing *sva-dharma* with the desire to teach people is the *pariniṣṭhita*. Both accept *āśramas*. The person whose heart has been purified by truth, austerity, and *japa*, etc., who is fully devoted to the Lord and without *āśrama* is the *nirapekṣa*.

The Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa is the *vācya,* to be discussed in the text. The *Gītā* is the *vācaka,* what expresses this. *Kṛṣṇa-tattva* is the only subject or *viṣaya*. The only goal (*prayojana*) of the scripture is directly meeting *kṛṣṇa-tattva,* while destroying unlimited suffering.

The explanation of *tattva* takes place in steps. First, the conversation between Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna amid the armies at Kurukṣetra is described as follows.

### Text 1

*dhṛtarāṣṭra uvāca—*  
*dharma-kṣetre kuru-kṣetre samavetā yuyutsavaḥ \|*  
*māmakāḥ pāṇḍavāś caiva kim akurvata sañjaya \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**1. Dhṛtarāṣṭra said: O Sañjaya, what did my sons Duryodhana and others and the sons of Pāṇḍu, Yudhiṣṭhira and others, do, having gathered with the desire to fight at Kurukṣetra, the place of** ***dharma*****?**

### Texts 2-3

*sañjaya uvāca—*  
*dṛṣṭvā tu pāṇḍavānīkaṁ vyūḍhaṁ duryodhanas tadā \|*  
*ācāryam upasaṅgamya rājā vacanam abravīt \|\|*  
*paśyaitāṁ pāṇḍu-putrāṇām ācārya mahatīṁ camūm \|*  
*vyūḍhāṁ drupada-putreṇa tava śiṣyeṇa dhīmatā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**2. Sañjaya said: Seeing the array of the Pāṇḍavas’ troops, King Duryodhana approached his teacher Droṇa and spoke these words.**

**3. “O teacher! See this great army of the sons of Pāṇḍu, arranged for battle by your disciple, intelligent Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the son of Drupada.”**

### Texts 4-6

*atra śūrā maheṣvāsā bhīmārjuna-samā yudhi \|*  
*yuyudhāno virāṭaś ca drupadaś ca mahā-rathaḥ \|\|*  
*dhṛṣṭaketuś cekitānaḥ kāśirājaś ca vīryavān \|*  
*purujit kuntibhojaś ca śaibyaś ca nara-puṅgavaḥ \|\|*  
*yudhāmanyuś ca vikrānta uttamaujāś ca vīryavān \|*  
*saubhadro draupadeyāś ca sarva eva mahā-rathāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**4-6. Here are brave men, skillful with great bows, equal to Bhīma and Arjuna in battle: Sātyaki, Virāṭa, and Drupada, a** ***mahā-ratha,*** **Dhṛṣṭaketu, Cekitāna, the valorous Kāśīrāja, Purujit, Kuntibhoja and Śaibya, the best of men, mighty Yudhāmanyu, valiant Uttamaujā, Abhimanyu, son of Subhadrā, and the five sons of Draupadī, all** ***mahā-rathas*****.**

### Text 7

*asmākaṁ tu viśiṣṭā ye tān nibodha dvijottama \|*  
*nāyakā mama sainyasya saṁjñārthaṁ tān bravīmi te \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**7. O guru, I will glorify the names of the commanders of our army so you can completely understand.**

### Text 8

*bhavān bhīṣmaś ca karṇaś ca kṛpaś ca samitiṁ-jayaḥ \|*  
*aśvatthāmā vikarṇaś ca saumadattir tathaiva ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**8. There are yourself, Bhīṣma, Karṇa, Kṛpa, Aśvatthāmā, Vikarṇa, and Bhūriśravā, the son of Somadatta.**

### Text 9

*anye ca bahavaḥ śūrā mad-arthe tyakta-jīvitāḥ \|*  
*nānā-śastra-praharaṇāḥ sarve yuddha-viśāradāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**9. There are also many other brave men who are ready to give up their lives for my sake, skillful at fighting, armed with various missiles and weapons.**

### Text 10

*aparyāptaṁ tad asmākaṁ balaṁ bhīṣmābhirakṣitam \|*  
*paryāptaṁ tv idam eteṣāṁ balaṁ bhīmābhirakṣitam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**10. Our army protected by Bhīṣma is immeasurable, but their army, protected by Bhīma, is limited.**

### Text 11

*ayaneṣu ca sarveṣu yathā-bhāgam avasthitāḥ \|*  
*bhīṣmam evābhirakṣantu bhavantaḥ sarva eva hi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**11. Situated in your arrays in various divisions, you all should protect Bhīṣma.**

### Text 12

*tasya sañjanayan harṣaṁ kuru-vṛddhaḥ pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*siṁha-nādaṁ vinadyoccaiḥ śaṅkhaṁ dadhmau pratāpavān \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**12. Then the powerful elder among the Kurus, the grandfather Bhīṣma, in order to bring joy to Duryodhana, making the sound of a lion, blew his conch loudly.**

### Text 13

*tataḥ śaṅkhāś ca bheryaś ca paṇavānaka-gomukhāḥ \|*  
*sahasaivābhyahanyanta sa śabdas tumulo ’bhavat \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**13. Then conches, kettle drums, small drums, larger drums and trumpets were suddenly sounded, making a tumultuous roar.**

### Text 14

*tataḥ śvetair hayair yukte mahati syandane sthitau \|*  
*mādhavaḥ pāṇḍavaś caiva divyau śaṅkhau pradadhmatuḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**14. Then Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna, mounted on an excellent chariot yoked with white horses, blew their divine conches.**

### Texts 15-16

*pāñcajanyaṁ hṛṣīkeśo devadattaṁ dhanañjayaḥ \|*  
*pauṇḍraṁ dadhmau mahāśaṅkhaṁ bhīma-karmā vṛkodaraḥ \|\|*  
*anantavijayaṁ rājā kuntī-putro yudhiṣṭhiraḥ \|*  
*nakulaḥ sahadevaś ca sughoṣa-maṇipuṣpakau \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**15-16. Kṛṣṇa, Hṛṣikeśa, blew Pāñcajanya, Arjuna blew Devadatta, and Bhīma of fearful deeds blew the great conch Pauṇḍra. King Yudhiṣṭhira, son of Kuntī, blew Ananta-vijaya, and Nakula and Sahadeva blew their conches Sughoṣa and Manipuṣpaka.**

### Texts 17-18

*kāśyaś ca parameṣvāsaḥ śikhaṇḍī ca mahā-rathaḥ \|*  
*dhṛṣṭadyumno virāṭaś ca sātyakiś cāparājitaḥ \|\|*  
*drupado draupadeyāś ca sarvaśaḥ pṛthivī-pate \|*  
*saubhadraś ca mahā-bāhuḥ śaṅkhān dadhmuḥ pṛthak pṛthak \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**17-18. O king Dhṛtarāṣṭra! Then the king of Kāśī, the greatest archer, as well as Śikhaṇḍī, a** ***mahā-ratha*****, Dhṛṣṭadyumna, Virāṭa and Sātyaki, who could not be defeated, Drupada, the five sons of Draupadī, and mighty-armed Abhimanyu, son of Subhadrā, blew their conches separately.**

### Text 19

*sa ghoṣo dhārtarāṣṭrāṇāṁ hṛdayāni vyadārayat \|*  
*nabhaś ca pṛthivīṁ caiva tumulo ’bhyanunādayan \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**19. That tumultuous sound of conches, filling the sky and earth, split the hearts of Dhṛtarāṣṭra’s sons.**

### Texts 20-23

*atha vyavasthitān dṛṣṭvā dhārtarāṣṭrān kapi-dhvajaḥ \|*  
*pravṛtte śastra-saṁpāte dhanur udyamya pāṇḍavaḥ \|\|*  
*hṛṣīkeśaṁ tadā vākyam idam āha mahīpate \|*  
*senayor ubhayor madhye rathaṁ sthāpaya me ’cyuta \|\|*  
*yāvad etān nirīkṣe ’haṁ yoddhu-kāmān avasthitān \|*  
*kair mayā saha yoddhavyam asmin raṇa-samudyame \|\|*  
*yotsyamānān avekṣe ’haṁ ya ete ’tra samāgatāḥ \|*  
*dhārtarāṣṭrasya durbuddher yuddhe priya-cikīrṣavaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**20-23. O King, then, as the armies prepared to fight, Arjuna with Hanumān’s insignia on his flag, seeing the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra standing there, ready to release his weapons, spoke to Hṛṣīkeśa:**

**O Acyuta please station my chariot between the two armies, so that I can view at the commencement of the war those with whom I should fight among those with a desire to fight. I see here persons gathered with a desire to fight, desiring to please Duryodhana.**

### Texts 24-25

*sañjaya uvāca—*  
*evam ukto hṛṣīkeśo guḍākeśena bhārata \|*  
*senayor ubhayor madhye sthāpayitvā rathottamam \|\|*  
*bhīṣma-droṇa-pramukhataḥ sarveṣāṁ ca mahī-kṣitām \|*  
*uvāca pārtha paśyaitān samavetān kurūn iti \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**24-25. Sañjaya said: O Dhṛtarāṣṭra! Having been addressed by Arjuna, conqueror of sleep, in this way, Hṛṣīkeśa, stationing the best of chariots between the two armies, spoke: O Arjuna! See these Kurus gathered here for war headed by Bhīṣma, Droṇa and others.**

### Text 26

*tatrāpaśyat sthitān pārthaḥ pitṝn atha pitāmahān \|*  
*ācāryān mātulān bhrātṝn putrān pautrān sakhīṁs tathā \|\|*  
*śvaśurān suhṛdaś caiva senayor ubhayor api \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**26. Arjuna saw fathers, grandfathers, teachers, maternal uncles, brothers, sons, grandsons, friends, fathers-in-law and well-wishers situated there on both sides.**

### Text 27

*tān samīkṣya sa kaunteyaḥ sarvān bandhūn avasthitān \|*  
*kṛpayā parayāviṣṭo viṣīdann idam abravīt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**27. The son of Kuntī, Arjuna, seeing all his relatives and friends situated there, overcome with compassion and depression, which had not occurred previously, spoke as follows.**

### Texts 28-29

*arjuna uvāca—*  
*dṛṣṭvemān svajanān kṛṣṇa yuyutsuṁ samupasthitam \|*  
*sīdanti mama gātrāṇi mukhaṁ ca pariśuṣyati \|\|*  
*vepathuś ca śarīre me roma-harṣaś ca jāyate \|*  
*gāṇḍīvaṁ sraṁsate hastāt tvak caiva paridahyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**28-29. Arjuna said: O Kṛṣṇa, seeing all my relatives and friends gathered, desiring to fight, my limbs are weakening, my mouth is drying up, my body is trembling, and my hairs are standing on end. My bow is falling from my hand and my skin is burning.**

### Text 30

*na ca śaknomy avasthātuṁ bhramatīva ca me manaḥ \|*  
*nimittāni ca paśyāmi viparītāni keśava \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**30. I cannot stand anymore, and my mind is confused. I see ill omens indicating contrary results, O Keśava.**

### Text 31

*na ca śreyo ’nupaśyāmi hatvā svajanam āhave \|*  
*na kāṅkṣe vijayaṁ kṛṣṇa na ca rājyaṁ sukhāni ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**31. I do not see any good result from killing my own people in the war. And I do not desire victory or the happiness of a kingdom, O Kṛṣṇa.**

### Texts 32-35

*kiṁ no rājyena govinda kiṁ bhogair jīvitena vā \|*  
*yeṣām arthe kāṅkṣitaṁ no rājyaṁ bhogāḥ sukhāni ca \|\|*  
*ta ime ’vasthitā yuddhe prāṇāṁs tyaktvā dhanāni ca \|*  
*ācāryāḥ pitaraḥ putrās tathaiva ca pitāmahāḥ \|\|*  
*mātulāḥ śvaśurāḥ pautrāḥ śyālāḥ saṁbandhinas tathā \|*  
*etān na hantum icchāmi ghnato ’pi madhusūdana \|\|*  
*api trailokya-rājyasya hetoḥ kiṁ nu mahīkṛte \|*  
*nihatya dhārtarāṣṭrān naḥ kā prītiḥ syāj janārdana \|\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**32-35. O Govinda! What is the use of kingdom, enjoyment or even living? Those for whom we desire kingdom, enjoyment and happiness they are situated here, determined for battle, giving up their lives and wealth. I do not desire to kill teachers, fathers, sons and grandfathers, maternal uncles, fathers-in-law, grandsons, brothers-in-law and relatives, even if I were to be killed by them. O Madhusūdana.**

**Even for sovereignty over the three worlds, what to speak of this earth, O Janārdana, what happiness will we achieve, having killed the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra?**

### Text 36

*pāpam evāśrayed asmān hatvaitān ātatāyinaḥ \|*  
*tasmān nārhā vayaṁ hantuṁ dhārtarāṣṭrān sabāndhavān \|*  
*svajanaṁ hi kathaṁ hatvā sukhinaḥ syāma mādhava \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**36. In killing these aggressors, though permitted by political scriptures, we will only incur sin, because it is contrary to dharma scriptures to kill aggressors who are gurus. Therefore, we should not kill the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra with their relatives. O Mādhava, how can we be happy, having killed our own relatives?**

### Texts 37-38

*yady apy ete na paśyanti lobhopahata-cetasaḥ \|*  
*kula-kṣaya-kṛtaṁ doṣaṁ mitra-drohe ca pātakam \|\|*  
*kathaṁ na jñeyam asmābhiḥ pāpād asmān nivartitum \|*  
*kula-kṣaya-kṛtaṁ doṣaṁ prapaśyadbhir janārdana \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**37-38. Duryodhana and others, their intelligence overcome by greed, do not see the fault of destroying the family and the sin in killing friends. But should not we, who see the fault in destroying the family, withdraw from this sin, O Janārdana?**

### Text 39

*kula-kṣaye praṇaśyanti kula-dharmāḥ sanātanāḥ \|*  
*dharme naṣṭe kulaṁ kṛtsnam adharmo ’bhibhavaty uta \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**39. With the destruction of the family, the eternal,** ***dharma*** **or proper conduct of the family is destroyed. When that conduct is destroyed, the remaining family is completely overcome by improper conduct.**

### Text 40

*adharmābhibhavāt kṛṣṇa praduṣyanti kula-striyaḥ \|*  
*strīṣu duṣṭāsu vārṣṇeya jāyate varṇa-saṅkaraḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**40. From the prevalence of** ***adharma*** **or improper conduct, the women of the family become contaminated. O Kṛṣṇa, crown of the Vṛṣṇi dynasty! When the women are spoiled, mixed** ***varṇas*** **arise*****.***

### Text 41

*saṅkaro narakāyaiva kula-ghnānāṁ kulasya ca \|*  
*patanti pitaro hy eṣāṁ lupta-piṇḍodaka-kriyāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**41. Mixed** ***varṇas*** **in the family brings hell for the family and for those who have destroyed their family members. The forefathers fall, being deprived of** ***piṇḍa*** **and water offerings.**

### Text 42

*doṣair etaiḥ kula-ghnānāṁ varṇa-saṅkara-kārakaiḥ \|*  
*utsādyante jāti-dharmāḥ kula-dharmāś ca śāśvatāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**42. Both** ***varṇa*** **and eternal family conduct of the killers of the family are destroyed by this fault of mixed** ***varṇas*****.**

### Text 43

*utsanna-kula-dharmāṇāṁ manuṣyāṇāṁ janārdana \|*  
*narake niyataṁ vāso bhavatīty anuśuśruma \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**43. O Janārdana, we have heard that those men whose family conduct are destroyed live in hell permanently.**

### Text 44

*aho bata mahat pāpaṁ kartuṁ vyavasitā vayam \|*  
*yad rājya-sukha-lobhena hantuṁ sva-janam udyatāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**44. We are intent on committing great sin, since we are ready to kill our own people for gain of happiness of a kingdom.**

### Text 45

*yadi mām apratīkāram aśastraṁ śastra-pāṇayaḥ \|*  
*dhārtarāṣṭrā raṇe hanyus tan me kṣemataraṁ bhavet \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**45. It would be better for me if the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, weapons in hand, were to kill me without weapons and without resistance.**

### Text 46

*sañjaya uvāca—*  
*evam uktvārjunaḥ saṅkhye rathopastha upāviśat \|*  
*visṛjya saśaraṁ cāpaṁ śoka-saṁvigna-mānasaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**46. Speaking in this manner, Arjuna, giving up his bow and arrows, mind disturbed with lamentation, sat down on his chariot.**

## Chapter Two - Ātmānātmā-viveka-yoga

### Text 1

*sañjaya uvāca—*  
*taṁ tathā kṛpayāviṣṭam aśru-pūrṇākulekṣaṇam \|*  
*viṣīdantam idaṁ vākyam uvāca madhusūdanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**1. Sañjaya said: Then Madhusūdana spoke the following words to Arjuna, whose eyes were filled with tears, and who was overcome with compassion, his face full of sorrow.**

### Text 2

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca—*  
*kutas tvā kaśmalam idaṁ viṣame samupasthitam \|*  
*anārya-juṣṭam asvargyam akīrti-karam arjuna \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**2. The Lord said: O Arjuna! How has this bewilderment which is against** ***dharma*****, precluding Svarga in next life and fame in this life, come upon you at the time of battle?**

### Text 3

*klaibyaṁ mā sma gamaḥ pārtha naitat tvayy upapadyate \|*  
*kṣudraṁ hṛdaya-daurbalyaṁ tyaktvottiṣṭha parantapa \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**3. O son of Kuntī! Do not become a coward. This is not suitable for you. O afflicter of enemies! Give up this low weakness of heart. Rise up and fight!**

### Text 4

*arjuna uvāca—*  
*kathaṁ bhīṣmam ahaṁ saṅkhye droṇaṁ ca madhusūdana \|*  
*iṣubhiḥ pratiyotsyāmi pūjārhāv ari-sūdana \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**4. Arjuna said, “O Madhusūdana, O killer of enemies, in the battle, how will I be able to fire arrows against worshippable Bhīṣma and Droṇa, my guru?**

### Text 5

*gurūn ahatvā hi mahānubhāvāñ*  
*śreyo bhoktuṁ bhaikṣyam apīha loke \|*  
*hatvārtha-kāmāṁs tu gurūn ihaiva*  
*bhuñjīya bhogān rudhira-pradigdhān \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**5. It is better to eat by begging in this life, than killing such great elders. Even if I kill elders out of desire for wealth, I will enjoy objects contaminated with their blood.**

### Text 6

*na caitad vidmaḥ kataran no garīyo*  
*yad vā jayema yadi vā no jayeyuḥ \|*  
*yān eva hatvā na jijīviṣāmas*  
*te ’vasthitāḥ pramukhe dhārtarāṣṭrāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**6. I cannot understand which is better: whether we should conquer them or whether they should conquer us? These sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, by killing whom we do not wish to live, whether we should conquer them or whether they should conquer us, are situated facing us.**

### Text 7

*kārpaṇya-doṣopahata-svabhāvaḥ*  
*pṛcchāmi tvāṁ dharma-saṁmūḍha-cetāḥ \|*  
*yac chreyaḥ syān niścitaṁ brūhi tan me*  
*śiṣyas te ’haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**7. My courageous nature has been overcome by weakness. My mind is bewildered about dharma. In this condition, I ask you what is best. Please tell me with certainty. I am your student. I am surrendered unto you. Please instruct me.**

### Text 8

*na hi prapaśyāmi mamāpanudyād*  
*yac chokam ucchoṣaṇam indriyāṇām \|*  
*avāpya bhūmāv asapatnam ṛddhaṁ*  
*rājyaṁ surāṇām api cādhipatyam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**8. I do not see any method which can remove this sorrow, which is drying up my senses, even if I attain an unrivalled prosperous kingdom on earth and even sovereignty over** ***devatās*****.**

### Text 9

*sañjaya uvāca—*  
*evam uktvā hṛṣīkeśaṁ guḍākeśaḥ parantapaḥ \|*  
*na yotsya iti govindam uktvā tūṣṇīṁ babhūva ha \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**9. Sañjaya said: Having thus spoken to Hṛṣīkeśa, Guḍākeśa, afflicter of enemies, saying, “Govinda, I will not fight,” became silent.**

### Text 10

*tam uvāca hṛṣīkeśaḥ prahasann iva bhārata \|*  
*senayor ubhayor madhye viṣīdantam idaṁ vacaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**10. O Dhṛtarāṣṭra! Hṛṣīkeśa, smiling slightly, spoke the following words to the depressed Arjuna, amidst the two armies.**

### Text 11

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca—*  
*aśocyān anvaśocas tvaṁ prajñā-vādāṁś ca bhāṣase \|*  
*gatāsūn agatāsūṁś ca nānuśocanti paṇḍitāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**11. \[In order to show one does not become qualified for** ***sannyāsa*** **by temporary renunciation arising from lamentation,\] the Lord said: O Arjuna! You are lamenting for what is not worthy of lamentation, while speaking words like a wise person. The wise do not lament for the dead or the living.**

### Text 12

*na tv evāhaṁ jātu nāsaṁ na tvaṁ neme janādhipāḥ \|*  
*na caiva na bhaviṣyāmaḥ sarve vayam ataḥ param \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**12. {The** ***ātma*** **is indestructible. Thus, there is no reason to lament. There are two types of** ***ātma:*** **Paramātmā and** ***jīvātmā*****. I am Paramātmā and you and all the kings are** ***jīvātmās*****. It is not that in the past I, you and all the kings did not exist, and in the future will not exist. We all were existing in the past, exist now and will exist in the future.}**

**12. \[In order to make understand the knowledge of** ***ātmā*** **and non*****-ātmā,*** **first the Lord speaks of a common quality of the Paramātmā and the** ***jīvātmā,*** **both being** ***ātmā*****.\]** ***Ātmā*** **is indestructible, and thus not a cause of lamentation. There are two types of** ***ātmā:*** **Paramātmā and** ***jīvātmā*****. I am Paramātmā and you and these kings are** ***jīvātmā*****. It is not that I, you and all the kings did not previously exist and that will not exist in the future. We exist now, we existed in the past and will exist in the future.**

### Text 13

*dehino ’smin yathā dehe kaumāraṁ yauvanaṁ jarā \|*  
*tathā dehāntara-prāptir dhīras tatra na muhyati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**13. {Just as when, possessing a body, in this body successively one passes through infancy, youth and old age, and the** ***ātmā*** **exists, so when the body ends, the existence of the** ***ātmā*** **is not destroyed. Rather, just as after the stage of infancy, one becomes joyful on attaining youth, so on giving up the aged body, the** ***ātmā*** **of the devotee attains joy and excellence. Thus, the wise person does not lament at the destruction of the body.}**

**13. \[Now, indicating the** ***jīva*** **bound by matter, the Lord speaks. Just as when, possessing a body, in this body successively one passes through infancy, youth and old age, and the** ***ātmā*** **exists, so when the body ends, the existence of the** ***ātmā*** **is not destroyed. Thus, the wise person does not lament at the destruction of the body.\]**

### Text 14

*mātrā-sparśās tu kaunteya śītoṣṇa-sukha-duḥkha-dāḥ \|*  
*āgamāpāyino ’nityās tāṁs titikṣasva bhārata \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**14. Winter and summer, which give happiness or suffering, experienced by as objects of the senses (*****mātrā*****), are impermanent. O son of Kuntī! Tolerating all this is the** ***dharma*** **prescribed by the scriptures. The natural dharma of the** ***kṣatriyas*** **is fighting. If you give up this dharma, eventually great obstacles will appear.**

**14. By the function of the senses (*****mātrā*****), one experiences contact with sense objects (*****sparśa*****). That contact with sense objects is cold and hot, which generate happiness and suffering. They come and go. Thus they are temporary. Tolerating all this is the dharma prescribed by the scriptures.**

### Text 15

*yaṁ hi na vyathayanty ete puruṣaṁ puruṣarṣabha \|*  
*sama-duḥkha-sukhaṁ dhīraṁ so ’mṛtatvāya kalpate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**15. O best of men, the intelligent person, equal in happiness and distress, who is not pained by heat and cold, is qualified for immortality or liberation \[in the form of really perfecting himself.\]**

### Text 16

*nāsato vidyate bhāvo nābhāvo vidyate sataḥ \|*  
*ubhayor api dṛṣṭo ’ntas tv anayos tattva-darśibhiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**16. Lamentation and illusion, qualities of what is not the ātmā, take shelter of the body. They have no existence in the jīva, whose svarūpa is ātmā. There is no destruction of the jīva, which has an eternal svarūpa. Thus, those who see the truth, distinguishing between the eternal and temporary, determine the truth about them. Thus, the bodies of Bhīṣma and others, who are jīvas (ātma-svarūpa), are temporary. Their ātmās cannot be destroyed since that is their very nature.**

**16. The material body is asat and moreover subject to change and thus temporary. The jīvātmā is sat, i.e. not subject to change, and thus eternal. One cannot destroy the jīva with unchanging svarūpa. Thus the seers of truth, distinguishing the unchanging from the changing, deliberate on the truth.**

### Text 17

*avināśi tu tad viddhi yena sarvam idaṁ tatam \|*  
*vināśam avyayasyāsya na kaścit kartum arhati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**17. The indestructible** ***jīva*** **spreads throughout all the body of man in the form of** ***ātmā*****. Though it is very small in size, it has the ability to pervade the whole body, just as a powerful medicine spreads it nourishment through the whole body. It is called** ***sarva-ga*** **(going everywhere) since it can wander in Svarga, in hell and in various wombs. It is imperishable (*****avyaya*****) or eternal. No one can destroy it.**

**17. The indestructible** ***jīva*** **spreads throughout all bodies of man in the form of** ***ātmā*****. Though it is very small, it has the ability to pervade the whole body, just as a powerful medicine spreads it nourishment through the whole body. It is imperishable (*****avyaya*****) or eternal. No one can destroy it.**

### Text 18

*antavanta ime dehā nityasyoktāḥ śarīriṇaḥ \|*  
*anāśino ’prameyasya tasmād yudhyasva bhārata \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**18. All these bodies are temporary but the** ***jīvātmā*** **in the body is indestructible. This** ***jīva*** **or** ***jīvātmā*** **cannot be measured since it is very small. Thus O Bhārata! Not giving up** ***sva-dharma*** **enjoined in scriptures, fight.**

**18. All these bodies are temporary but the** ***jīva*** **is immeasurable, indestructible, eternal and embodied. Thus not lamenting for the body, fight in order to follow the conduct of** ***dharma,*** **which is a cause for liberation.**

### Text 19

*ya enaṁ vetti hantāraṁ yaś cainaṁ manyate hatam \|*  
*ubhau tau na vijānīto nāyaṁ hanti na hanyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**19. He who thinks that one** ***jīva*** **kills another** ***jīvātmā*** **and that one** ***jīva*** **is killed by another** ***jīvātmā*** **does not know anything. The** ***jīvātmā*** **does not kill anyone and it cannot be killed by anyone. O my friend Arjuna! You are** ***ātmā*****. You are not a killer and cannot be killed. {There is no need to fear that you will gain infamy from the ignorant as a killer of elders.}**

### Text 20

*na jāyate mriyate vā kadācin*  
*nāyaṁ bhūtvā bhavitā vā na bhūyaḥ \|*  
*ajo nityaḥ śāśvato ’yaṁ purāṇo*  
*na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**20. The** ***jīvātmā*** **is unborn (*****aja*****) or without birth. It is eternal (*****nitya*****), existing at all times. It cannot be destroyed in the three times of past, present and future. It has no birth or death. It is not born again and again and does not grow. It is ancient, and moreover eternally fresh. It cannot be killed. It has no substantial relationship with the body which is prone to birth and death.**

**20. The** ***jīvātmā*** **is devoid of the six transformations. It is without birth (*****aja*****), existing at all times (*****nitya*****). It has no birth or death and moreover it does not take birth, increase, etc. It is ancient and moreover ever youthful. It is not killed by separation from the body which is subject to birth and death.**

### Text 21

*vedāvināśinaṁ nityaṁ ya enam ajam avyayam \|*  
*kathaṁ sa puruṣaḥ pārtha kaṁ ghātayati hanti kam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**21. O Arjuna! He who knows that** ***jīva*** **is indestructible, eternal, unborn, and imperishable—whom does he kill? Whom does he order to kill?**

**21. O Arjuna! He who knows constantly that this soul is indestructible, unborn, and imperishable—who does he kill and whom does he order to kill by any means at all?**

### Text 22

*vāsāṁsi jīrṇāni yathā vihāya*  
*navāni gṛhṇāti naro ’parāṇi \|*  
*tathā śarīrāṇi vihāya jīrṇāny*  
*anyāni saṁyāti navāni dehī \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**22. Just as people give up old clothes and accepts new ones, so the embodied soul gives up the old bodies and attains new ones.**

### Text 23

*nainaṁ chindanti śastrāṇi nainaṁ dahati pāvakaḥ \|*  
*na cainaṁ kledayanty āpo na śoṣayati mārutaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**23. The** ***jīvātmā*** **is not cut by any weapon. It is not burned by fire. It is not moistened by water nor dried by the wind.**

### Texts 24-25

*acchedyo ’yam adāhyo ’yam akledyo ’śoṣya eva ca \|*  
*nityaḥ sarva-gataḥ sthāṇur acalo ’yaṁ sanātanaḥ \|\|*  
*avyakto ’yam acintyo ’yam avikāryo ’yam ucyate \|*  
*tasmād evaṁ viditvainaṁ nānuśocitum arhasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**24-25. The soul cannot be cut, cannot be burned, cannot be moistened or dried. It is eternal, going everywhere \[i.e. wandering in all forms\], fixed in definition and immovable {i.e. durable}. It is always existing (*****sanātana*****).**

**It is said to be invisible, inconceivable and unchanging. {Since it is very small, it is called invisible. Still since it has qualities like pervading the body, it is called inconceivable. Since it is devoid of the six transformations, it called unchanging.} Understanding the** ***jīvātmā*** **to be like this, you should give up lamentation.**

### Text 26

*atha cainaṁ nitya-jātaṁ nityaṁ vā manyase mṛtam \|*  
*tathāpi tvaṁ mahābāho nainaṁ śocitum arhasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**26. O mighty-armed one! Even if you think the** ***jīva*** **is always born and always dies, \[as the Lokāyatas and Vaibhāṣika Buddhists believe,\] still you should not lament. \[If you lament you are lower than these low philosophers.\]**

### Text 27

*jātasya hi dhruvo mṛtyur dhruvaṁ janma mṛtasya ca \|*  
*tasmād aparihārye ’rthe na tvaṁ śocitum arhasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**27. \[Consider the philosophy of the logicians.\] If one is born, death is certain. If one dies, birth is certain in order to experience results of karma. Therefore, since both are unavoidable, you should not lament. \[You will be lower than the logicians if you are agitated with lamentation.\]**

### Text 28

*avyaktādīni bhūtāni vyakta-madhyāni-bhārata \|*  
*avyakta-nidhanāny eva tatra kā paridevanā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**28. O Bhārata! Unmanifested beings, being born, become visible. {In the continuous time} between birth and death, they are visible and when they die, they become invisible. Therefore, why lament? Even if this is not accepted by the wise, still accepting it by consideration, you should fight to protect** ***kṣatriya-dharma*****.**

### Text 29

*sāścarya-vat paśyati kaścid enam*  
*āścarya-vad vadati tathaiva cānyaḥ \|*  
*āścarya-vac cainam anyaḥ śṛṇoti*  
*śrutvāpy enaṁ veda na caiva kaścit \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**29. Some see the** ***jīvātmā*** **with amazement. Some speak of it with amazement. And some hear \[the truth\] about it with amazement. {Knowing and} hearing about it \[in plenty\], some cannot understand it. From this mistaken concept concerning** ***jīva’s svarūpa*** **arises obstacles such as materialism, the theory of temporary conscious beings and pure monism.**

### Text 30

*dehī nityam avadhyo ’yaṁ dehe sarvasya bhārata \|*  
*tasmāt sarvāṇi bhūtāni na tvaṁ śocitum arhasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**30. O Bhārata, actually {though it passes out of the body}, the soul remains without the possibility of being killed. Therefore, you should not lament for any being.**

### Text 31

*sva-dharmam api cāvekṣya na vikampitum arhasi \|*  
*dharmyād dhi yuddhāc chreyo ’nyat kṣatriyasya na vidyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**31. Concerning your** ***sva-dharma*****, you should not fear, because there is no better** ***karma*** **than fighting according to dharma for the** ***kṣatriya*****. \[The reason is by that one protects citizens, punishes the wicked, and maintains the earth with dharma.\] There are two types of** ***sva-dharma*** **for the** ***jīva:*** **in the bound state and in the liberated state. In the liberated state, the** ***jīva’s sva-dharma*** **is without** ***upādhis*****. In the bound state the** ***jīva’s sva-dharma*** **is to some degree endowed with** ***upādhis*****. In the bound condition, the** ***jīva*** **has various intermediate states. In these intermediate states, different forms of** ***sva-dharma*** **are unavoidable. When the** ***jīva*** **is situated in a human body, if his** ***sva-dharma*** **takes the form of** ***varṇāśrama-dharma*** **it is good. Therefore, another name for** ***varṇāśrama-dharma*** **is** ***sva-dharma*****. For a person who has a** ***kṣatriya*****-nature, what can be better than fighting?**

### Text 32

*yadṛcchayā copapannaṁ svarga-dvāram apāvṛtam \|*  
*sukhinaḥ kṣatriyāḥ pārtha labhante yuddham īdṛśam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**32. O Pārtha! Warriors who obtain such a battle which comes by chance and which opens the doors to heaven are {happy}/\[fortunate\].**

### Text 33

*atha cet tvam imaṁ dharmyaṁ saṁgrāmaṁ na kariṣyasi \|*  
*tataḥ sva-dharmaṁ kīrtiṁ ca hitvā pāpam avāpsyasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**33. Therefore, if you do not take part in this righteous war, deviating from your** ***dharma*** **and fame, you will instead reap sin \[in the form of rejecting** ***sva-dharma*****\].**

### Text 34

*akīrtiṁ cāpi bhūtāni kathayiṣyanti te ’vyayām \|*  
*saṁbhāvitasya cākīrtir maraṇād atiricyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**34. If this happens, the people will broadcast your infamy for a long time. For one who has a great position, dishonor is more miserable than death.**

### Text 35

*bhayād raṇād uparataṁ maṁsyante tvāṁ mahā-rathāḥ \|*  
*yeṣāṁ ca tvaṁ bahu-mato bhūtvā yāsyasi lāghavam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**35. The great warriors, by whom you were regarded highly, will now regard you with contempt. They will think that you have abandoned the battlefield out of fear.**

### Text 36

*avācya-vādāṁś ca bahūn vadiṣyanti tavāhitāḥ \|*  
*nindantas tava sāmarthyaṁ tato duḥkhataraṁ nu kim \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**36. Your enemies will speak many sharp words about you, criticizing your abilities. What could be more painful than this?**

### Text 37

*hato vā prāpsyasi svargaṁ jitvā vā bhokṣyase mahīm \|*  
*tasmād uttiṣṭha kaunteya yuddhāya kṛta-niścayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**37. O son of Kuntī! Being killed, you will attain heaven. Being victorious, you will enjoy the earth. Therefore, rise, and fight with determination.**

### Text 38

*sukha-duḥkhe same kṛtvā lābhālābhau jayājayau \|*  
*tato yuddhāya yujyasva naivaṁ pāpam avāpsyasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**38. Being equal in happiness and distress, in gain and loss, in victory and defeat, \[desiring liberation or on the path of liberation,\] you will not incur any sin while fighting the battle.**

### Text 39

*eṣā te ’bhihitā sāṅkhye buddhir yoge tv imāṁ śṛṇu \|*  
*buddhyā yukto yayā pārtha karma-bandhaṁ prahāsyasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**39. Intelligence related to** ***jñāna*** **(*****sāṅkhya*****) has been explained. Now, hear about intelligence related to** ***bhakti-yoga*****. O Pārtha! Endowed with intelligence related to** ***bhakti*** **it is possible to destroy** ***saṁsāra*****.**

**Комментарий:**

Later it will be explained that there is only one yoga of intelligence (*buddhi-yoga*). When this *buddhi-yoga* is characterized by limits of *karma,* it is called *karma-yoga*. When it surpasses the limits of *karma,* and reaches the limit of *jñāna,* it is called *jñāna-yoga* or *sāṅkhya-yoga*. When it surpasses the limits of both, it touches on *bhakti.* Thus, it is called *bhakti-yoga,* or pure, complete *buddhi-yoga*.

**Перевод:**

**39. Intelligence related to separate** ***tattvas*** **of** ***sāṅkhya*** **i.e.** ***jñāna*** **and and** ***sva-dharma*** **has been explained. Now, hear about intelligence related to both of them. O Pārtha! Endowed with intelligence related to yoga, it is possible to destroy** ***saṁsāra*****.**

**Комментарий:**

Later it will be explained that there is only one yoga connecting *karma, jñāna* and *bhakti*. When this yoga is characterized by limits of *karma*, it is called *karma-yoga*. When it surpasses the limits of *karma*, and reaches the border of *jñāna,* it is called *jñāna-yoga* or *sāṅkhya-yoga*. When it surpasses the limits of both, and touches on *bhakti,* it is called *bhakti-yoga,* or *buddhi-yoga* or complete yoga.

All *tattva* was separately explained through *sāṅkhya-jñāna*. From verse 21 to 30 *ātmā-tattva* was explained and from verse 31 to 38 non-*ātmā-tattva* in the form of *sva-dharma* was explained. Next the combination of both will be explained. By the yoga of both, finally the true perfection of *ātmā* will be described.

### Text 40

*nehābhikrama-nāśo ’sti pratyavāyo na vidyate \|*  
*svalpam apy asya dharmasya trāyate mahato bhayāt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**40.There is no loss even in just beginning this {*****bhakti-*****}*****yoga*** **and there is no decrease in it. Performing it even a little frees the performer from the greatest fear in the form of** ***saṁsāra*****.**

### Text 41

*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana \|*  
*bahu-śākhā hy anantāś ca buddhayo ’vyavasāyinām \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**41. There are two types of bhakti-yoga: primary bhakti-yoga consists of hearing, chanting, etc., and secondary bhakti-yoga consisting of niṣkāma-karma offered to Kṛṣṇa.**

**I am the only goal of primary bhakti-yoga . Thus, intelligence related to this is fixed (vyavasyāyātmikā ). Persons who are not solely dedicated to me (avyavasāyinām) have intelligence related to karma-yoga . Since this becomes fixed in many objects, it becomes many branched and aims at unlimited desires. There is doubt about its ability to destroy karma and incur loss.**

**41. Aiming for the perfection of the real nature of ātmā, the intelligence in karma-yoga which produces jñāna-yoga is one. That is called vyavasāyātmikā buddhi . The intelligence of unfixed people concentrates on kāmya-karmas . It is many branched since it is fixed on many objects, aiming at unlimited desires. In that there is doubt about its ability to destroy karma and incur loss.**

### Text 42

*yām imāṁ puṣpitāṁ vācaṁ pravadanty avipaścitaḥ \|*  
*veda-vāda-ratāḥ pārtha nānyad astīti vādinaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**42. These unfixed people are ignorant. \[Concluding that there is no truth except matter,\] they are always engaged in speaking about the Vedas—they are absorbed in interpreting the meaning and not understanding the chief purport of the Vedas.**

### Text 43

*kāmātmānaḥ svarga-parā janma-karma-phala-pradām \|*  
*kriyā-viśeṣa-bahulāṁ bhogaiśvarya-gatiṁ prati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**43.{They are filled with desire for ordinary** ***karmas*****. They pray for heaven. They are attached to speaking flowery words, attractive to hear (which change into poison), which encourage acts for attaining power and enjoyment—words which give their actions’ results in the form of higher birth.}**

**43.\[They are filled with desire for results of** ***kāmya-karmas.*** **They pray for heaven. They are attached to speaking flowery words, attractive to hear (which change into poison), for attaining power and enjoyment by many acts which give results of karma. Repeatedly they say these words.\]**

### Text 44

*bhogaiśvarya-prasaktānāṁ tayāpahṛta-cetasām \|*  
*vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ samādhau na vidhīyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**44. For those foolish people, lacking discrimination, who are attached to happiness of enjoyment and power, their intelligence does not attain** ***samādhi*****, full concentration on the Lord.**

**44. For those foolish people, lacking discrimination, who are attached to happiness of enjoyment and power, lacking** ***samādhi*****, their intelligence does not concentrate on the Lord, since their minds have been stolen by the many flowery words.**

### Text 45

*traiguṇya-viṣayā vedā nistraiguṇyo bhavārjuna \|*  
*nirdvandvo nitya-sattva-stho niryoga-kṣema ātmavān \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**45. There are two types of subjects in all scriptures: those which are** ***uddiṣṭa*** **and those which are** ***nirdiṣṭa*****.**

**Those topics which are the ultimate conclusion are called** ***uddiṣṭa*****. Those topics which are explained, aiming at the ultimate conclusion are called** ***nirdiṣṭa*****. Arundhatī star is the** ***uddiṣṭa*** **and the larger stars near it which are first pointed out are the** ***nirdiṣṭa*****.**

**In all the Vedas, the** ***tattva*** **beyond the** ***guṇas*** **is the** ***uddiṣṭa*****. But since that is difficult to immediately designate, first some** ***tattva*** **endowed with** ***guṇas*** **is presented. Thus, at first glance it seems that the topic of all the Vedas is** ***māyā*****, consisting of the three** ***guṇas*** **of** ***sattva, rajas*** **and** ***tamas*****.**

**O Arjuna! Not being bound up by these** ***nirdiṣṭa*** **topics, after attaining the** ***uddiṣṭa tattva,*** **accept what is devoid of the three** ***guṇas*****. In some places the Vedas teach** ***karma*** **endowed with** ***rajas*** **and** ***tamas*****. In some places they teach** ***jñāna,*** **endowed with** ***sattva*****. And in special places they teach** ***bhakti*** **beyond the** ***guṇas*****.**

**{Being devoid of duality of respect and disrespect within the** ***guṇas,*** **associating with my devotees (*****nitya-sattva*****), giving up gain and preservation sought on the paths of** ***jñāna*** **and** ***karma,*** **attain the position beyond the** ***guṇas*** **along with** ***buddhi-yoga*****.}**

**\[Being devoid of duality of respect and disrespect within the** ***guṇas*** **being situated in the nature of pure** ***ātmā*** **(*****nitya-sattva-sthaḥ*****), giving up gain and preservation, attain the position beyond the** ***guṇas*** **along with** ***buddhi-yoga.*****\]**

### Text 46

*yāvān artha udapāne sarvataḥ saṁplutodake \|*  
*tāvān sarveṣu vedeṣu brāhmaṇasya vijānataḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**46. Small water bodies like wells are called udapāna and huge water bodies are called samplutodaka. One can perform actions such as bathing, washing clothes, etc., individually in different wells but in a large water body one can perform all those actions easily.**

**In one place in the Vedic scriptures, each devatā is described and the actions to be done regarding each devatā is described. But by deliberating on all the Vedas the brāhmaṇas, knowing the conclusion of the Vedas, determine that all results are obtained by me, Bhagavān alone. Those whose intelligence if fixed in this, naturally worship only Bhagavān.**

**46. Small water bodies like wells are called udapāna and huge water bodies are called samplutodaka. Activities such as bathing, drinking, etc., which can be performed in a large water body, can be performed in a small water body also. Similarly the brāhmaṇas who know the meaning of the Vedas use all the Vedas, and also perform actions to attain real understanding of ātmā by taking shelter of their own branch and Upaniṣads.**

### Text 47

*karmaṇy evādhikāras te mā phaleṣu kadācana \|*  
*mā karma-phala-hetur bhūr mā te saṅgo ’stv akarmaṇi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**47. Discernment concerning karmas is three kinds—karma, akarma and vikarma. Vikarma or sinful acts and akarma, avoidance of karmas related to one’s sva-dharma—these two are most inauspicious. You should not desire (saṅga) either of these. Giving up akarma {and vikarma}, you should perform karmas with attention.**

**Karma is of three types: nitya-karma, naimittika-karma and kāmya-karma. Of these, kāmya-karma produce inauspiciousness. Those who do kāmya-karmas  become the cause of results of karma (karma-phala-hetu).**

**Thus, I speak for your auspiciousness. Do not become the cause of producing karmic results on taking shelter of** ***kāmya-karmas*****. You have qualification for performing** ***karmas*** **according to your** ***sva-dharma*** **but you are not qualified for results of karma at all. Those who take support of {*****bhakti-*****}*****yoga,*** **accept** ***nitya*** **and** ***naimittika karmas*** **for maintaining their physical existence.**

### Text 48

*yogasthaḥ kuru karmāṇi saṅgaṁ tyaktvā dhanañjaya \|*  
*siddhy-asiddhyoḥ samo bhūtvā samatvaṁ yoga ucyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**48. Being situated in {*****bhakti-*****}*****yoga*****, perform your** ***sva-dharma*****, giving up desire for results. Being equal minded in attaining results of** ***karma*** **or not attaining results of** ***karma,*** **\[fixing the mind on that,\] is called** ***yoga*****.**

### Text 49

*dūreṇa hy avaraṁ karma buddhi-yogād dhanañjaya \|*  
*buddhau śaraṇam anviccha kṛpaṇāḥ phala-hetavaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**49. Cultivating** ***bhakti*** **by** ***niṣkāma-karma-yoga*** **(*****buddhi-yoga*****)—reject** ***kāmya-karmas*** **completely. Those who seek results from their work are wretched. Therefore, take shelter of** ***buddhi-yoga*****.**

**49. O Dhanañjaya! Take shelter of intelligence in the form of** ***niṣkāma-karma-yoga,*** **which produces understanding of true nature of** ***ātmā*** **while rejecting completely** ***kāmya-karmas*** **which are much inferior to** ***buddhi-yoga*****. Those who perform** ***kāmya-karmas*** **with desire for results are controlled by birth and karma (*****kṛpanāḥ*****).**

### Text 50

*buddhi-yukto jahātīha ubhe sukṛta-duṣkṛte \|*  
*tasmād yogāya yujyasva yogaḥ karmasu kauśalam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**50.** ***Buddhi-yoga*** **is skill in** ***karma*****. Thus, being endowed with intelligence, reject completely pious and sinful acts while in this world.**

### Text 51

*karmajaṁ buddhi-yuktā hi phalaṁ tyaktvā manīṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*janma-bandha-vinirmuktāḥ padaṁ gacchanty anāmayam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**51. Endowed with intelligence, rejecting all results arising from** ***karmas*****, the wise become free of bondage of birth and attain the {final}/\[suitable\] state of the devotees, the place of no suffering.**

### Text 52

*yadā te mohakalilaṁ buddhir vyatitariṣyati \|*  
*tadā gantāsi nirvedaṁ śrotavyasya śrutasya ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**52. Practicing** ***niṣkāma-karma*** **offered to the Lord, when your intelligence gives up completely the forest of illusion, then you will be indifferent to all scriptures heard and to be heard and {will engage in practicing pure** ***bhakti*****}.**

### Text 53

*śruti-vipratipannā te yadā sthāsyati niścalā \|*  
*samādhāv acalā buddhis tadā yogam avāpsyasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**53. When your intelligence is no longer agitated by various meanings of the** ***Vedas*****, then situated in natural** ***samādhi*****, you will attain {pure** ***bhakti-yoga*****}/\[*****buddhi-yoga*** **consisting of the three** ***tattvas*** **of** ***niṣkāma-karma,*** **pure** ***jñāna*** **and** ***bhakti*** **to the Lord\].**

### Text 54

*arjuna uvāca—*  
*sthita-prajñasya kā bhāṣā samādhi-sthasya keśava \|*  
*sthita-dhīḥ kiṁ prabhāṣeta kim āsīta vrajeta kim \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**54. Having heard this much, Arjuna said: O Keśava, what are the characteristics of a person having fixed intelligence (*****sthita-prajña*****)? What does the** ***sthita-prajña*****, {situated in** ***samādhi,*** **or a** ***jīvanmukta*****} {say}/\[think or do\] when confronted with respect or disrespect, praise or criticism, affection or hatred? How does he act towards external objects, \[when rejecting or employing them\]? I wish to know all this.**

### Text 55

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca—*  
*prajahāti yadā kāmān sarvān pārtha mano-gatān \|*  
*ātmany evātmanā tuṣṭaḥ sthita-prajñas tadocyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**55. The Lord said: O Pārtha! When the** ***jīva*** **completely gives up all desires which arise from the mind, and is satisfied with seeing the** ***svarūpa*** **of the** ***ātmā*****, the** ***svarūpa*** **of bliss, with a withdrawn mind, he is called** ***sthita-prajña*****.**

### Text 56

*duḥkheṣv anudvigna-manāḥ sukheṣu vigata-spṛhaḥ \|*  
*vīta-rāga-bhaya-krodhaḥ sthita-dhīr munir ucyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**56. The sage whose mind is not disturbed by the appearance of suffering of body, mind or society, who does not desire happiness even when the various happiness of various sense objects are present, and who is devoid of attachment, fear and anger \[in one’s duties\], is called a** ***sthita-prajña or sthita-dhī*****.**

### Text 57

*yaḥ sarvatrānabhisnehas tat tat prāpya śubhāśubham \|*  
*nābhinandati na dveṣṭi tasya prajñā pratiṣṭhitā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**57. He who has no material affection, who does not rejoice on attaining good fortune or curse on attaining misfortune, his intelligence is fixed.**

**As long as the body remains, attainment or nonattainment of material objects is unavoidable but the sthita-prajña has no attraction to gain or repulsion to loss, for his intelligence is situated in samādhi.**

### Text 58

*yadā saṁharate cāyaṁ kūrmo ’ṅgānīva sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*indriyāṇīndriyārthebhyas tasya prajñā pratiṣṭhitā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**58. All the senses, becoming subserviant to external objects, want to wander about. But the senses of the sthita-prajña, being subserviant to intelligence cannot wander about independently for sense objects like sound. They require the permission of the intelligence to act.**

**Just as a turtle withdraws its limbs inside by its desire, the senses of the sthita-prajña, by the desire of the intelligence, are sometimes inactive and sometimes move towards sense objects.**

### Text 59

*viṣayā vinivartante nirāhārasya dehinaḥ \|*  
*rasa-varjaṁ raso ’py asya paraṁ dṛṣṭvā nivartate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**59. The process of withdrawing from sense objects by fasting seen in the case of embodied jīvas is an extremely foolish process. The practice of stopping the senses through yama, niyama, āsana, prāṇāyāma and pratyāhāra as established in aṣṭāṅga-yoga is the method for such people. But this rule is not accepted by those who are sthita-prajña. Attracted to the Lord on seeing his beauty, they give up ordinary material attraction to objects.**

**Though there is an arrangement for restraining the senses from sense objects by fasting for foolish people, there is no eternal auspiciousness for the jīva except rāga-marga \[directed to the supreme ātmā\]. When that rāga ttains an attractive object, it naturally gives up inferior objects.**

### Text 60

*yatato hy api kaunteya puruṣasya vipaścitaḥ \|*  
*indriyāṇi pramāthīni haranti prasabhaṁ manaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**60. This is because for those who attempt to free the heart from material attraction (*****rāga*****) by the path of** ***vidhi*** **(rules), their extremely agitating senses push the mind towards material objects now and then. There is no worry of falling on the path of** ***rāga*****.**

**60. Wise persons on the path of dry** ***jñāna*** **attempt to free the heart from material attraction by the path of indifference to matter. But still their extremely agitating senses push the mind towards material objects. There is no worry of falling on the path of** ***rāga*** **directed towards supreme** ***ātmā*****.**

### Text 61

*tāni sarvāṇi saṁyamya yukta āsīta mat-paraḥ \|*  
*vaśe hi yasyendriyāṇi tasya prajñā pratiṣṭhitā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**61. The person, who is situated on the path of** ***yoga*** **in the form of** ***yukta-vairāgya*** **described, performing** ***karma-yoga*** **aiming at pure*****bhakti,*** **controls all the senses appropriately, his intelligence is fixed.**

### Text 62

*dhyāyato viṣayān puṁsaḥ saṅgas teṣūpajāyate \|*  
*saṅgāt sañjāyate kāmaḥ kāmāt krodho ’bhijāyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**62. On the other hand, examine the anarthas of practice of phalgu-vairāgya on the path of vidhi.**

**62. While making attempts at renunciation when meditation on objects arises, gradually desire (saṅga) for the object takes place. From that lust arises and  from lust anger manifests.**

### Text 63

*krodhād bhavati saṁmohaḥ saṁmohāt smṛti-vibhramaḥ \|*  
*smṛti-bhraṁśād buddhi-nāśo buddhi-nāśāt praṇaśyati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**63. From anger arises bewilderment. From bewilderment comes loss of memory. From loss of memory comes destruction of intelligence. From destruction of intelligence, one becomes completely destroyed.**

**In many circumstances, this is the progress of phalgu-vairāgya. Thus, this type of yoga is filled with obstacles.**

### Text 64

*rāga-dveṣa-vimuktais tu viṣayān indriyaiś caran \|*  
*ātma-vaśyair vidheyātmā prasādam adhigacchati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**64. Taking support of the** ***yoga*** **of** ***yukta-vairāgya,*** **while giving up attachment and hatred {through fixed intelligence}, suitably engaging the senses, subservient to** ***ātmā,*** **to all sense objects, the independent person (*****vidheyātmā*****), attains satisfaction of the heart.**

### Text 65

*prasāde sarva-duḥkhānāṁ hānir asyopajāyate \|*  
*prasanna-cetaso hy āśu buddhiḥ paryavatiṣṭhate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**65. When there is satisfaction of the heart {i.e.** ***bhakti*****}, all suffering is destroyed. The intelligence of the devotee with satisfied heart \[quickly\] becomes fixed in his desired object completely.**

### Text 66

*nāsti buddhir ayuktasya na cāyuktasya bhāvanā\|*  
*na cābhāvayataḥ śāntir aśāntasya kutaḥ sukham \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**66. Notice this also. For the person without meditation on the highest rasa, how is it possible to have peace from inferior rasa? And how can the person  devoid of peace attain the highest happiness?**

**Thus, the intelligence of persons without proper engagement cannot meditate on the Lord, which is contemplation of the highest rasa.**

**66. The person without engagement in yoga cannot meditate on rasa. One cannot attain peace from material rasa, without mediation on the highest rasa. If one has no peace, how can one experience the highest happiness, bliss of ātmā?**

### Text 67

*indriyāṇāṁ hi caratāṁ yan mano ’nuvidhīyate \|*  
*tad asya harati prajñāṁ vāyur nāvam ivāmbhasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**67. The mind which wanders to sense objects, following one of the senses, steals away the intelligence of the inattentive person, just as wind makes a boat unsteady.**

**67. When the mind which wanders to sense objects following one sense among the senses, that one sense steals away the intelligence of the inattentive person, just as wind makes a boat on the water unsteady.**

### Text 68

*tasmād yasya mahā-bāho nigṛhītāni sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*indriyāṇīndriyārthebhyas tasya prajñā pratiṣṭhitā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**68. O mighty-armed one! Know that that person’s intelligence is called “fixed” who completely restricts the senses from the sense objects by** ***yukta-vairāgya.***

**68. Thus, O mighty-armed one! That person’s intelligence is fixed who completely restricts the senses from the sense objects by** ***yukta-vairāgya*****.**

### Text 69

*yā niśā sarva-bhūtānāṁ tasyāṁ jāgarti saṁyamī \|*  
*yasyāṁ jāgrati bhūtāni sā niśā paśyato muneḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**69. O Arjuna! There are two types of intelligence: that inclined to ātmā and that dedicated to material objects. The intelligence dedicated to ātmā**  
**is a type of night for ordinary jīvas (sarva-bhūta) bewildered by matter. All jīvas bewildered by matter are asleep in that night and cannot attain knowledge of ātmā . But the sthita-prajña \[sage\] remains awake in that night and directly experiences bliss fixed in intelligence for the ātmā.**

**\[On the other hand,\] in intelligence dedicated to matter, the** ***jīva*** **bewildered by matter is awake and {directly} experiences the lamentation and illusion in objects fixed in that intelligence. That is a type of night for the** ***sthita-prajña*** **sage.**

**{He, seeing indifferently all senses objects which give happiness and suffering for people in** ***saṁsāra,*** **accepts all sense objects for his enjoyment as much as necessary without being contaminated.}**

**\[He accepts all senses objects, which, pulled by** ***prārabdha-karma,*** **give happiness and suffering for people in** ***saṁsāra,*** **indifferently and as much as necessary without being contaminated.\]**

### Text 70

*āpūryamāṇam acala-pratiṣṭhaṁ*  
*samudram āpaḥ praviśanti yadvat \|*  
*tadvat kāmā yaṁ praviśanti sarve*  
*sa śāntim āpnoti na kāma-kāmī \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**70. A person full of desires can never be peaceful. Just as water, though entering the full ocean cannot disturb the ocean, \[similarly\] all desires, though entering the** ***sthita-prajña*** **sage, cannot disturb him. Thus he alone achieves peace.**

### Text 71

*vihāya kāmān yaḥ sarvān pumāṁś carati niḥspṛhaḥ \|*  
*nirmamo nirahaṅkāraḥ sa śāntim adhigacchati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**71. He who, giving up all material desires, without desire for any material object, moves about without** ***ahaṅkāra*** **and possessiveness, attains peace.**

### Text 72

*eṣā brāhmī sthitiḥ pārtha naināṁ prāpya vimuhyati \|*  
*sthitvāsyām anta-kāle ’pi brahma-nirvāṇam ṛcchati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**72. This situation is called brahmī sthiti. O Pārtha! He who attains this position does not become bewildered. At the time of death, like King Khaṭvāṅga, if he attains this position, he attains brahma-nirvāṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

{The situation of attaining Brahman is called *brahmī-sthiti*.} Liberation from matter, which leads to attainment of Brahman, is called *brahma-nirvāṇa*. The *tattva* different from matter is called Brahman. If one is situated in this *tattva,* one attains spiritual *rasa*.

This chapter is called *Gītā-sūtra:* summary of the *Gītā*, since in it *karma* and *jñāna* are clearly described and bhakti, which is their goal, is indistinctly described. The nature of the questioner is described till verse 10. From verse 11 to 30 there is a discussion on what is *ātmā* and what is not *ātmā*. From verse 31 to 38 there is a discussion one sin and piety within *karma-yoga,* which is the very form of *sva-dharma*. From verse 69 till the end of the chapter *niṣkāma-karma-yoga* for understanding the real nature of ātmā in the form of a combination of *jñāna* and *karma* previously discussed and the life and conduct of a person situated in *yoga* are discussed.

## Chapter Three - Karma-yoga

### Text 1

*arjuna uvāca—*  
*jyāyasī cet karmaṇas te matā buddhir janārdana \|*  
*tat kiṁ karmaṇi ghore māṁ niyojayasi keśava \|\| 1 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**1. Arjuna said: O Janārdana, O Keśava! If it is your opinion that fixed intelligence {directed to** ***bhakti*****} beyond the** ***guṇas,*** **is superior to** ***karma*****, then why do you permit me to engage in** ***karma*** **in the form of this terrible war \[for attaining intelligence\]?**

### Text 2

*vyāmiśreṇaiva vākyena buddhiṁ mohayasīva me \|*  
*tad ekaṁ vada niścitya yena śreyo ’ham āpnuyām \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**2. Just on hearing all the teachings you gave {to me}, it seems that there are many unconnected meanings. In some places you taught {*****nirguṇa-bhakti*** **attained by a devotees’ mercy}/\[*****ātma-jñāna*****\]. In other places you permitted me to perform** ***karmas,*** **after revealing my qualification for** ***karma*****. From this I must say that** ***sāttvika karma*** **is superior to** ***rājasika karma*****. And better than** ***sāttvika karma*** **is** ***jñāna*****.** ***Jñāna*** **is also a special** ***sāttvika karma*****.**

**{If I do not have qualification for attaining** ***nirguṇa-bhakti*** **give me teachings on** ***sāttvika karmas*****, i.e. on** ***jñāna,*** **by which I will become free from the bondage of** ***saṁsāra*****. It is better to give instructions on** ***karma*** **to a person qualified for** ***karma*****. Therefore, give instructions with decisive statements.}**

**\[Among the two, say definitely what is best for me.\]**

### Text 3

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca—*  
*loke ’smin dvi-vidhā niṣṭhā purā proktā mayānagha \|*  
*jñāna-yogena sāṅkhyānāṁ karma-yogena yoginām \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**3. The Lord said: I have not taught in the previous chapter that** ***sāṅkhya-yoga*** **and** ***karma-yoga*** **are both mutually independent methods for liberation. There is no method for liberation except {*****bhakti-yoga*****}/\[the** ***yoga*** **of the real nature of** ***ātmā*****\].**

**There are two types of** ***niṣṭhā*** **in the** ***sādhana*** **of {*****bhakti-yoga*****}/\[the** ***yoga*** **of the real nature of** ***ātmā*****\]. Those of pure heart, ascending to the level of** ***jñāna,*** **become fixed in** ***sāṅkhya-yoga*** **or** ***jñāna*****. Being fixed in** ***karma-yoga*** **in order to become pure in heart is not respected by them. By being fixed in** ***sāṅkhya-yoga*** **alone, they ascend to {*****bhakti-yoga*****}/\[*****yoga*** **of the real nature of** ***ātmā*****\].**

**Those who are not pure in heart, after ascending to** ***jñāna*** **level by** ***niṣkāma-karma-yoga*****, finally attain liberation {by** ***bhakti*****}/\[in terms of the real nature of** ***ātmā*****\]. Actually, the ladder to attain the level {of** ***bhakti*****}/\[of that\] is only one. There are two types of steadiness according to the state of the person ascending.**

### Text 4

*na karmaṇām anārambhān naiṣkarmyaṁ puruṣo ’śnute \|*  
*na ca sannyasanād eva siddhiṁ samadhigacchati \|\| 4 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**4. By avoiding performance of scriptural** ***karmas*****, a person cannot attain steadiness in** ***jñāna*** **in the form of** ***naiṣkarmya*****. Just by renunciation of scriptural karmas, how can an impure person attain perfection? (*****Rasika-rañjana*****)**

**4. By avoiding performance of prescribed** ***karmas*****, a person cannot attain steadiness in** ***jñāna*** **(*****naiṣkarmya*****). By renunciation of prescribed** ***karmas,*** **an impure person cannot attain perfection. (*****Vidvad-rañjana*****)**

### Text 5

*na hi kaścit kṣaṇam api jātu tiṣṭhaty akarma-kṛt \|*  
*kāryate hy avaśaḥ karma sarvaḥ prakṛti-jair guṇaiḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**5. Even if a person of impure heart gives up scriptural** ***karmas,*** **impelled by the** ***guṇas*** **of** ***prakṛti*****, he will do material activities, beyond his control. Therefore, one must not give up** ***karmas*** **prescribed in scripture, which purify the heart.**

### Text 6

*karmendriyāṇi saṁyamya ya āste manasā smaran \|*  
*indriyārthān vimūḍhātmā mithyācāraḥ sa ucyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**6. What will happen if a person of impure heart controls his action senses? Controlling the action senses, this person still thinks of the sense objects in his mind. Thus this fool is called a pretender.**

### Text 7

*yas tv indriyāṇi manasā niyamyārabhate ’rjuna \|*  
*karmendriyaiḥ karma-yogam asaktaḥ sa viśiṣyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**7. The person who, controlling all the senses by the mind, \[without attachment\] starts** ***karma-yoga*** **in householder** ***dharma*** **by the action senses, is better than the pretender \[mentioned previously\], though he is incapable, {since by doing** ***karma-yoga,*** **he gradually becomes capable of controlling the senses, while giving up desire for results}.**

### Text 8

*niyataṁ kuru karma tvaṁ karma jyāyo hy akarmaṇaḥ \|*  
*śarīra-yātrāpi ca te na prasidhyed akarmaṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**8. For an unqualified person, karma is better than renouncing karma. When one cannot even maintain the body by giving up your karmas, how is it possible to give up karmas?**

**Thus, while, giving up kāmya-karmas, \[fight, protect the citizens, and\] performing nitya-karmas like sandhya-upāsana, etc., when the heart becomes pure, {surpassing the stage of jñāna you will attain nirguṇa-bhakti}/\[attaining the stage of jñāna, you will attain ātmā as it is\].**

### Text 9

*yajñārthāt karmaṇo ’nyatra loko ’yaṁ karma-bandhanaḥ \|*  
*tad-arthaṁ karma kaunteya mukta-saṅgaḥ samācara \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**9. {*****Niṣkāma-dharma*** **offered to the Lord}/\[*****Niṣkāma-karma*** **to satisfy the Lord\] is called** ***yajña*****.** ***Karmas*** **should be performed for** ***yajña*****. Know that all other** ***karmas*** **except these are called bondage of karma. Do all** ***karmas*** **for the purpose of** ***yajña*****. Even** ***karmas*** **{performed for satisfaction of the Lord}/\[offered to the Lord\] with aim of personal desires are causes of bondage. Therefore, devoid of desire for results of** ***karma,*** **perform** ***karmas*** **{offered to the Lord}/\[for satisfying the Lord\]. {Such** ***karmas,*** **becoming a genuine means to** ***bhakti-yoga,*** **will produce** ***nirguṇa-bhakti,*** **having aroused knowledge of** ***bhagavat-tattva*****.}**

### Text 10

*saha-yajñāḥ prajāḥ sṛṣṭvā purovāca prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*anena prasaviṣyadhvam eṣa vo ’stv iṣṭa-kāma-dhuk \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**10. {A person of impure heart must perform** ***niṣkāma-karma*****. Renouncing** ***karma*** **is not beneficial for him. If one is not able to perform** ***niṣkāma-karma*****, one should perform** ***karmas*** **offered to the Lord, even with material desire. By no means should one perform** ***vikarma*** **(sinful acts) or** ***akarma*** **(giving up** ***karmas*****).}**

**Having created offspring along with** ***yajña,*** **\[at creation\] {Brahmā}/\[Prajāpati\] gave the order, “Taking shelter of this** ***dharma*** **in the form of** ***yajña,*** **prosper excellently. This** ***yajña*** **will bestow all your desires: \[*****ātmā-jñāna*** **which purifies the heart and liberation while maintaining your body\].”**

### Text 11

*devān bhāvayatānena te devā bhāvayantu vaḥ \|*  
*parasparaṁ bhāvayantaḥ śreyaḥ param avāpsyatha \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**11. By this** ***yajña*** **all the** ***devatās*** **will be pleased with you. When the** ***devatās*** **are pleased, they will give desired results to you.**

**11. By this** ***yajña*** **please all the** ***devatās*** **who are my limbs. When the** ***devatās*** **are pleased, they will show their pleasure by giving desired results to you. When this exchange takes place, you will attain real nature of** ***ātmā*****, the highest benefit.**

### Text 12

*iṣṭān bhogān hi vo devā dāsyante yajña-bhāvitāḥ \|*  
*tair dattān apradāyaibhyo yo bhuṅkte stena eva saḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**12. Not giving to the** ***devatās*** **by the five** ***mahā-yajñas*****, food which arises by rain which is supplied by the** ***devatās,*** **and simply enjoying the food, you became full of fault, and a thief.**

### Text 13

*yajña-śiṣṭāśinaḥ santo mucyante sarva-kilbiṣaiḥ \|*  
*bhuñjate te tv aghaṁ pāpā ye pacanty ātma-kāraṇāt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**13. Those who accept food which is remnants of sacrifice are freed from all sin, which is unavoidable in endeavors. Those who enjoy food selfishly experience all sinful results, having committed sin.**

### Text 14

*annād bhavanti bhūtāni parjanyād anna-saṁbhavaḥ \|*  
*yajñād bhavati parjanyo yajñaḥ karma-samudbhavaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**14. All creatures arise from food. Food arises from rain. Rain arises from** ***yajña*****.** ***Yajña*** **arises from** ***karmas*****.**

### Text 15

*karma brahmodbhavaṁ viddhi brahmākṣara-samudbhavam*  
*tasmāt sarva-gataṁ brahma nityaṁ yajñe pratiṣṭhitam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**15.** ***Karmas*** **arises from the** ***Vedas*** **(*****brahma*****). The** ***Vedas*** **arise from the Lord (*****akṣara*****). Therefore,** ***yajña,*** **the cause of the movement of the universal wheel, must be performed by those qualified to do so. The all-pervading Brahman is eternally situated in the sacrifice.**

### Text 16

*evaṁ pravartitaṁ cakraṁ nānuvartayatīha yaḥ \|*  
*aghāyur indriyārāmo moghaṁ pārtha sa jīvati \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**16. He who, qualified for {kāmya-} karma, does not follow this cycle which moves the wheel of the universe, being a servant of his senses and engaging in a sinful life, leads a useless life.**

**Комментарий:**

The purport is that one does not experience sin or piety by {*niṣkāma-karma-yoga* offered to the Lord}/\[*bhakti-yoga*, a form of the real nature of *ātmā*\] since this path is described in the scriptures as the excellent path for attaining *nirguṇa-bhakti*. The person who takes shelter of this path easily becomes purified in his heart, destroying all contamination.

Those who do not attain qualification for {*niṣkāma-karma-yoga,* offered to the Lord}/\[*bhakti-yoga*\] are always controlled by desire and sense satisfaction. They are engaged in sin. In order to curb their sinful tendency, the only method is pious *karmas*. When a sin occurs, one must take support of atonement. The arrangement for *yajña* is *dharma* or *puṇya karma*. That which produces auspiciousness for *samaṣṭi jīvas* and enables the universal wheel to move is called *puṇya*. By these *puṇyas* all unavoidable sins such as *pañca-sūnā* are destroyed. What is accepted by the performer for his own happiness and senses satisfaction, while maintaining the auspiciousness of the universe, that, {becoming an *aṅga* of *yajña*}/\[terminating in *yajña*\], is counted among the *puṇyas*.

Those who produce auspicious results for the universe through unseen rules are special *devatās* arising from the Lord’s *śakti.* Pleasing these *devatās,* the form of the rules, when one attains pleasures given by their mercy, there is no sin involved. This is called the wheel of *karma*. Accepting *karmas* by worship of *devatās* in this way is called *kāmya-karma* offered to the Lord. Those who enact all these rules as rules related to matter are mere moral performers of *karma*. They are not performers of *karmas* offered to the Lord. Thus performing *kāmya-karmas* offered to the Lord in the proper way produces auspiciousness for the qualified *jīva*.

### Text 17

*yas tv ātma-ratir eva syād ātma-tṛptaś ca mānavaḥ*  
*ātmany eva ca santuṣṭas tasya kāryaṁ na vidyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**17. All** ***jīvas*** **existing in this type of wheel of** ***karma*** **are obligated to perform** ***karmas*** **necessarily. But those who are able to distinguish between** ***ātmā*** **and non-*****ātmā*****, they become absorbed in** ***ātmā*****, become satisfied in** ***ātmā*** **and things related to** ***ātmā*****.**

**17. All** ***jīvas*** **existing in this type of wheel of** ***karma*** **are obligated to perform** ***karmas*** **necessarily. But those who are able to distinguish between** ***ātmā*** **and non-*****ātmā*** **and become absorbed in things related to** ***ātmā*** **(*****ātma-ratiḥ*****), they become satisfied in** ***ātmā*** **and things related to** ***ātmā*****.**

**Комментарий:**

They do not perform karmas as obligatory. Performing karmas merely for supporting the body, they seek peace in the form of release from the wheel of karma. Though doing all actions, they do not perform nitya, \[naimittika\] and kāmya karmas. Thus, their actions are not called “ karmas.” The actions being differently situated, are called jñāna \[-yoga\] and even bhakti \[-yoga\].

### Text 18

*naiva tasya kṛtenārtho nākṛteneha kaścana \|*  
*na cāsya sarva-bhūteṣu kaścid artha-vyapāśrayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**18. {For the person who has experienced bliss of** ***ātmā*****,** ***puṇya*** **through obligatory** ***karmas*** **and sin through non-performance of obligatory** ***karmas*** **does not take place. He does not take shelter of any selfish actions occurring among all beings from Brahmā down to plants. Satisfied with the pleasure of** ***ātmā*****, he has no goal in sin or** ***puṇya*****. Whatever he does or does not do by his nature, all is auspicious.}**

**18. For the person who has experienced bliss of** ***ātmā,*** **there is no gain through performance of obligatory** ***karmas*** **and no loss through non-performance of obligatory** ***karmas*****. He does not take shelter of anyone among all beings such as** ***devatās*** **or humans to attain his goals since he has attained the highest goal in the form of realization of** ***ātmā*****. Whatever he does or does not do by his nature, all is auspicious. In this state, performing some** ***karmas*** **or not performing them is seen.**

### Text 19

*tasmād asaktaḥ satataṁ kāryaṁ karma samācara*  
*asakto hy ācaran karma param āpnoti pūruṣaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**19. Always perform karmas without attachment to their results, since by performing karmas without attachment, a person attains liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Liberation is nothing but only the mature state of all karmas, the highest bhakti.

**Перевод:**

**19. Therefore, always perform karmas without attachment to their results, since by performing karmas without attachment, a person attains the supreme tattva in the form of realization of ātmā.**

### Text 20

*karmaṇaiva hi saṁsiddhim āsthitā janakādayaḥ*  
*loka-saṅgraham evāpi sampaśyan kartum arhasi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**20. By performance of** ***karmas,*** **Janaka and others, qualified for** ***jñāna,*** **attained {success in the form of** ***bhakti*****}/\[realization of** ***ātmā*****\]. To teach others, you should perform** ***karmas*****.**

### Text 21

*yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas tat tad evetaro janaḥ \|*  
*sa yat pramāṇaṁ kurute lokas tad anuvartate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**21. Whatever actions the superior person performs, other persons of lesser level will also follow. What a person accepts as correct conduct, others follow.**

### Text 22

*na me pārthāsti kartavyaṁ triṣu lokeṣu kiñcana \|*  
*nānavāptam avāptavyaṁ varta eva ca karmaṇi \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**22. O son of Pṛthā! I am the Supreme Lord (*****īśvara*****). For me, there is nothing in the three worlds that has to be done \[and whatever is worthy of attaining is not unattained for me\]. But I still perform** ***karmas*****.**

### Text 23

*yadi hy ahaṁ na varteyaṁ jātu karmaṇy atandritaḥ \|*  
*mama vartmānuvartante manuṣyāḥ pārtha sarvaśaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**23. If I were to reject** ***karmas*****, though being fully alert, all men would follow me and reject** ***karmas*****.**

### Text 24

*utsīdeyur ime lokā na kuryāṁ karma ced aham \|*  
*saṅkarasya ca kartā syām upahanyām imāḥ prajāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**24. If I were not to perform** ***karmas*****, all people would become degraded, also giving up** ***karmas*****. Consequently, I would be the cause of confusion of rules and would destroy the progeny.**

### Text 25

*saktāḥ karmaṇy avidvāṁso yathā kurvanti bhārata \|*  
*kuryād vidvāṁs tathāsaktaś cikīrṣur loka-saṅgraham \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**25. Thus, just as the ignorant perform karmas with attachment, O Bhārata, the learned, to teach the people, should perform karmas without attachment.**

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, the karmas of the learned and ignorant are not different. Know that only the strength of their attachment or non-attachment is different.

### Text 26

*na buddhi-bhedaṁ janayed ajñānāṁ karma-saṅginām \|*  
*joṣayet sarva-karmāṇi vidvān yuktaḥ samācaran \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**26. This is the purport of** ***karma*****. The person who does not know that** ***jñāna*** **produces** ***bhakti*** **is ignorant. By that ignorance, {he accepts other desires, in the form of intermediate results}/\[he becomes attached to** ***karmas*****\]. Thus, he is associated with** ***karma*** **(*****karma-saṅgī*****).**

**{When one speaks the conclusion concerning** ***tattva-jñāna*** **to the ignorant and to the** ***karma-saṅgī*****, he does not reveal any enthusiasm for it with faith.}**

**\[When one speaks the conclusion of** ***jñāna*** **which produces** ***bhakti,*** **to the ignorant** ***karma-saṅgī*****, he does not reveal any enthusiasm for it with faith.\]**

**Thus, the learned person, not giving instructions to give up material** ***karmas*** **suddenly to that person, teaches him** ***karma*** **to purify his heart, while he himself performs** ***karmas,*** **employing** ***niṣkāma-karma-yoga*****. If one attempts to disrupt his intelligence suddenly, it will not be auspicious for that person.**

**\[But\] I give this instruction to those who teach** ***jñāna*****. This is not the instruction for those who teach** ***bhakti*** **since** ***bhakti*** **does not depend on purification of the** ***antaḥkaraṇa*****. I will deliberate on this in detail later.**

### Text 27

*prakṛteḥ kriyamāṇāni guṇaiḥ karmāṇi sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*ahaṅkāra-vimūḍhātmā kartāham iti manyate \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**27. Hear about difference between the person in knowledge and the person with no knowledge, \[though they are the same in performing** ***karmas*****\].**

**Bound to material** ***prakṛti*** **by ignorance, the** ***jīva*****, {under bewilderment of}/\[illusioned by\] material** ***ahaṅkāra,*** **thinks that all the actions – which are actually performed by the** ***guṇas*** **of** ***prakṛti*** **\[and by the supervision of the Lord\] – are {performed by him only}/\[his own actions\]. He thinks, “I am the doer of all these actions.” This is the quality of the person with no knowledge.**

### Text 28

*tattvavit tu mahā-bāho guṇa-karma-vibhāgayoḥ \|*  
*guṇā guṇeṣu vartanta iti matvā na sajjate \|\|*
